# Proposed OEIS comments: A184541 to A188821

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A184541

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A184541>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [5073](../papers/05001-05500/05073-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/24)*n^4 + (3/4)*n^3 + (383/24)*n^2 + (201/4)*n + 22.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Apr 12 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A184556

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A184556>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [11866](../papers/11501-12000/11866-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*a(n-1) + 7*a(n-2) - 23*a(n-3) - 4*a(n-4) + 12*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(50 + 36*x - 199*x^2 - 24*x^3 + 108*x^4) / ((1 + 2*x)*(1 - 8*x + 9*x^2 + 5*x^3 - 6*x^4)). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 27 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 9 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A184566

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A184566>

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. ```

## A185369

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A185369>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [12767](../papers/12501-13000/12767-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: +2*a(n) +4*(-n+1)*a(n-1) +2*(n-1)*(n-3)*a(n-2) +(n-1)*(n-2)*a(n-3)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The e.g.f. above is not algebraic, but it lies in a finitely generated module over Q(x) spanned by powers of a logarithm times powers of an exponential, and that module is closed under differentiation, so a(n) is P-recursive. Reducing the conjectured operator inside it leaves a polynomial, and the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A185966

Paper: [13756](../papers/13501-14000/13756-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 6*n*(n+1)*a(n) -n*(n-14)*a(n-1) +2*n*(14*n-19)*a(n-2) -4*(n-2)*(17*n-48)*a(n-3) +6*(2*n-5)*(n-4)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 15 2012

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A186120

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A186120>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [5110](../papers/05001-05500/05110-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - 6*a(n-3) + 6*a(n-4) - 4*a(n-5) for n>6.
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x^2*(7 - 12*x - 2*x^2 + 4*x^3 - 8*x^4) / ((1 - 2*x)*(1 - x - 2*x^2 + 2*x^3 - 2*x^4)). - _Colin Barker_, Apr 17 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 26 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 2 972 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 62. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A187028

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A187028>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [5741](../papers/05501-06000/05741-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 240*n^2 - 936*n + 912 for n>3

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. The count is a walk count in a finite graph, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; the conjectured form satisfies the same one, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to agree from then on, for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```

## A187252

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A187252>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [12774](../papers/12501-13000/12774-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence a(n) +(-2*n-3)*a(n-1) +(n+7)*(n-1)*a(n-2) +4*(-n^2+2*n+1)*a(n-3) +4*(n-3)^2*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 22 2022

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The e.g.f. above is not algebraic, but it lies in a finitely generated module over Q(x) spanned by powers of a logarithm times powers of an exponential, and that module is closed under differentiation, so a(n) is P-recursive. Reducing the conjectured operator inside it leaves a polynomial, and the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A187309

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A187309>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [10875](../papers/10501-11000/10875-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 7*a(n-1) + a(n-2) - 6*a(n-3) - 6*a(n-4) - 27*a(n-5) +90*a(n-6) - 81*a(n-8).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(210 + 2*x - 252*x^2 - 210*x^3 - 477*x^4 + 2718*x^5 - 324*x^6 - 2592*x^7) / (1 - 7*x - x^2 + 6*x^3 + 6*x^4 + 27*x^5 - 90*x^6 + 81*x^8). - _Colin Barker_, Apr 23 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 73 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A187588

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A187588>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [5746](../papers/05501-06000/05746-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 202*n^2 - 912*n + 994 for n>3.
> Empirical G.f.: 2*x^3*(53+130*x+34*x^2-15*x^3)/(1-x)^3. [_Colin Barker_, Jan 22 2012]

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A188707

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A188707>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [5891](../papers/05501-06000/05891-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```
